Previously: v2.6.27 (2026-07-27) — **The local join path had a reference client and the collab path did not, so an adopter built one and missed five contracts at once (Constellation v2.4.107)** — no `AckProcessed` (their targeted messages closed as `RelayUnreachable` while they were receiving), HELLO without identifying fields, `AgentHello` omitted because the no-rebroadcast wording read as forbidding it, no single-instance guard, and `History` appended verbatim to 594 MB. Contracts 1–4 carried from `join-local.cjs` unchanged — the guard existed in-repo since 2026-06-07 and did not reach adopters because only the local path reached for it.

Previously: v2.6.26 (2026-07-27) — **The previous cut left candidates on the table, and inside the same session that cost a real message (Constellation v2.4.106)** — `SpecGapReport` was a listed candidate with 11 occurrences on the wire; an adopter sent a product proposal under that name and the turn-end probe answered "nothing meaningful" while it sat unprocessed. Six names added with a reason each, and the measurement surfaced what a list cannot absorb: an adopter minted a date-stamped single-use intent name.

Previously: v2.6.25 (2026-07-27) — **The server had been reporting our undelivered messages all along, and the filter classified that as not meaningful (Constellation v2.4.105)** — `RelayUnreachable{msgId, targetAgentId, attemptCount, lastError}` sat unread in the inbox because it was not in the §13.16.9 allowlist; 22 non-deliveries accumulated and the loop was closed by the counterparty reporting the silence. Three more names added on measured evidence, and the set turned out to exist in four copies with the manual one six names behind.

Previously: v2.6.24 (2026-07-27) — **A validation that never ran reported `ok: true`, and a dependency nobody called was still advertised (Hyperbrief v0.7.4 · Ultrasafe v0.2.7)** — `hyperbrief_validate` answered `ok: true` when `ajv` was absent; it disclosed the skip in a secondary field, but the field a caller gates on is `ok`, so an IR that was never checked read as valid. Plugins ship as a code copy with no install step, so every declared npm dependency is absent in the only configuration an adopter runs. Now refuses by naming the package, and Ultrasafe's never-called ajv declaration was evicted with all five surfaces that asserted it corrected.

Previously: v2.6.23 (2026-07-26) — **Three of the four tool servers answered with nothing to render, and the tools themselves were fine (Compendium v0.2.10 · Hyperbrief v0.7.3 · Ultrasafe v0.2.6)** — `tools/call` returned the handler's result object with no `content` array, which is not a protocol error but a valid success carrying nothing a host can render: the call reports no output and the tool looks absent rather than broken. Wrapped at one dispatch choke point per server, because per-tool wrapping is how the defect spread. Verified live after the plugin pin moved — both layers of the silence confirmed resolved.

Previously: v2.6.22 (2026-07-26) — **The vocabulary module was completely non-functional when installed, and said nothing about it (Compendium v0.2.9)** — The store was located by walking two directories up from the runtime's own file: right inside the source repo, wrong in the only configuration an adopter runs. That directory exists, so the existence guard passed and only the subdirectories were missing — every tool answered empty with no error anywhere. A store is now recognized by its contents rather than by its directory existing, and an unresolved store is a refusal naming `COMPENDIUM_STORE` and listing every location tried rather than an empty result.

Previously: v2.6.21 (2026-07-26) — **The lifetime is finally asked about, and the renewal path survived it (Constellation v2.4.104)** — Enforcement, taken only after every lapsed key on the deployment had been renewed, so it disconnected nobody. One shared predicate is called from **both** sites a key is resolved at (connection upgrade for query-string keys, HELLO for body keys — v2.4.101 is the record of patching one and testing with an input that carried the key in both), and the upgrade refusal precedes `SERVER_HELLO`/`AgentList`/`History` because a URL-keyed client receives the board before it ever sends HELLO. Most importantly, refusal must **not** set `revokedAt` — that field means an operator ended the key and is the one condition renewal refuses, so recording expiry as revocation would make every aged-out key permanently unrenewable, silently, discovered only by an operator failing to fix an outage the enforcement itself caused.

Previously: v2.6.20 (2026-07-26) — **Enforcing a lifetime nobody could renew would have been an outage (Constellation v2.4.103)** — Three keys on the deployment were already past a 14-day window the admission path never consulted, so turning the check on would have arrived as a simultaneous three-way disconnection. The renewal path shipped first: expiry derives from the current window (`renewedAt` opens it, `issuedAt` immutable, `expiresAt` carried on the wire because `issuedAt + ttl` is wrong on any renewed key), renewal restarts the window **without changing the key string** so no secret is re-delivered and nobody is told to reconnect, and the asymmetry is the security content — a key lapsed by TTL alone may be renewed, a key an operator explicitly revoked never may, with `lapsed` and `revokedAt` on `KeyList` so consumers branch on the cause rather than on one rendered state. `keyRef` finally made `local`-kind keys addressable: they never appear in wire responses by design, so no canonical verb could select them, and the longest-lapsed key was exactly that kind.

Previously: v2.6.19 (2026-07-26) — **The counter had no writer, and the key found nothing (Ultrasafe v0.2.5)** — `.ultrasafe/state.json` carried 60+ detection events with `"iterations": []`: the hook only appends events, the gate only *reads* the array, and nothing ever wrote it — so the blocking-mode criterion "advisory evidence has accumulated" read as **satisfied** while the count had never moved. `ultrasafe_iteration_record` is the missing writer, following the consumer's contract with byte-identical state anchoring, `clean` **derived** rather than declared, an undiffable iteration recorded as **`null` not `0`**, and a missing state file **refused rather than created**. Separately, exact `file × line × pattern_id` dedup produced **zero** cross-axis matches across 52 findings: the line number in the key meant any edit made every finding in that file new, so the gate read improvement as regression. Iteration 1 is now recorded retroactively with every number read rather than restated (52 findings, coverage 0.54 below the 0.8 floor, `clean: false`, `iterations_run: 1` against ≥ 3).

Previously: v2.6.15 (2026-07-26) — **The invariant said identity binds by declaration. Nothing checked that the declaration was true (Constellation v2.4.99)** — §13.30.9's addressing ladder was sound and its precondition was absent: the wire held no relationship between the identity a connection claimed and the credential it presented, so resolution worked exactly as written and landed on a forger. **§13.25.11** supplies the missing layer — a declaration must be authenticated before it can be bound — and the takeover chain was reproduced end to end rather than inferred. `main` becomes a host-local station, `local`-kind keys are refused from remote, keys bind to an identity on first use, the envelope's `agentId`/`source` are overwritten from the authenticated connection, state-changing POSTs require same-origin signals, the board-state stream drops `ACAO: *`, and `/join/local` — which handed an unauthenticated remote caller the main's id and a worker's role description for a guessed label — goes host-local. TTL enforcement is recorded and deliberately deferred: switching it on would disconnect three live keys in one step.

Previously: v2.6.14 (2026-07-26) — **The room knew who could speak but not who had been spoken to (Constellation v2.4.98)** — §13.30.4 answered *who may speak* and nothing answered *who was spoken to*; the two are orthogonal, and conflating them makes a second agent answer a question handed to the first. **§13.30.9** supplies the missing axis: addressing binds by **declaration, never by resemblance**, over a fail-closed ladder (structured `addressee[]` → `replyTo` → an exactly-matched registered handle → nothing, terminal), with three cases that feel like being addressed and are not, gateway-side normalization as a duty, and a **monitoring stance** for the not-named case where silence is the output and only four named triggers justify interjecting. **§13.32.6** covers reception itself — *installation, not production*, split by one rule: default-mount pure discipline, require an explicit request for anything that runs a process, binds a port, spends money, or claims authority. Conduct therefore binds before infrastructure, and bootstrap precedes the first deliverable including on non-code projects.

Previously: v2.6.13 (2026-07-26) — **Recorded is not surfaced: 17 utterances sat in an inbox having never woken anyone (Constellation v2.4.97)** — v2.6.12 closed the case where an untargeted utterance never reaches the main agent. Replaying this deployment’s accumulated inbox through the new coalescer exposed a second silence on the path where it *does* arrive — and this one had been running the whole time. **51 external text frames → 17 utterances, 3,470 characters, zero wakes, ever.** A **targeted** text stream is relayed and written to the inbox faithfully; but `TEXT_MESSAGE_*` frames carry no `name`, and every consumer that gates on `name` — the wake filter, the §13.16.9 meaningful-inbound allowlist, the MCP meaningful set — dropped them without a word. Four of the seventeen were substantive (a contract-status confirmation, a routing-hardening verification, a cross-track status report); the operator had relayed each one by hand after noticing it on the dashboard, which is exactly the manual step this whole line of work exists to remove. Nothing was lost permanently and there is no backlog to work — the value is prospective. The lesson is the one the measurement names: **a name-gated pipeline turns a missing field into silence rather than an error.** So §13.13.3(e) makes the coalesced record a *named* envelope and requires `AgentText` registered in every name-gated consumer, on the same rule any new declaration event follows — producer and consumer filters ship in one cut, or the producer ships alone and nothing changes. This cut is the consumer half v2.6.12 should have carried: it was shipped a producer short, which is the exact lapse the rule exists to prevent. The two live bridges are a **parallel implementation**, not a copy of the reference one, so a fix to the reference reaches neither. Rather than hand-porting the logic twice — which starts the drift on the same day, invisibly — the coalescing semantics now live in one shared module both require, the same move already made for glob semantics. Verified by replay against real accumulated traffic rather than only against frames of my own construction: a synthetic smoke shows the contract holds, a replay shows what was actually being lost. Also calibrated, since the same replay measures it: **13 of the 17 were the peer’s own lifecycle chatter** rendered as text (*interrupting*, *working — 18 min*, *deduped a redelivery*, *watchdog draining*). A content-shaped classifier would be the wrong answer — fragile, and the volume is roughly eight utterances a month, so the noise costs less than the machinery. The structural fix belongs to the sender and already exists: status has names, and anything load-bearing should be an addressed `CUSTOM`.

Previously: v2.6.12 (2026-07-26) — **An utterance with no addressee still has a recipient, and none was listening (Constellation v2.4.96)** — A collaborating agent that simply *speaks* on a live board — a `TEXT_MESSAGE_*` stream with no `targetAgentId`, which is what an adapter emits when its author thinks of the board as a room rather than a mailbox — reached the boards and the history store and stopped there. The main agent’s inbox never saw it. Nothing errored, and the dashboard was correct precisely because it was the only consumer receiving the frames. Measured twice; the worse instance cost six hours, during which a peer’s substantive replies sat on screen until the operator said “check it”. **§13.13.3** gives the lane a recipient rule, as two halves that only work together: the server extends its target-unspecified fallback from `CUSTOM` to text frames, and the bridge — which returned on every non-`CUSTOM` frame — records them. **Gated on the sender’s declared role, not on frame shape.** The naive version re-creates a defect already paid for: a `local` sender is our own side, whose mirrors emit the identical frames continuously, and routing those to main is the false-wake cascade fixed in July with a `CUSTOM`-only guard on the worker’s poller — a guard this cut deliberately leaves in place. Frame shape cannot tell an utterance from an echo; only the declared identity of the sender can, which is §13.33.3-6 again: bind on what a party declared itself to be, never on what a payload resembles. The bridge coalesces the three frames into **one** record rather than relaying fragments — an agent left to reassemble a delta stream has been handed the bridge’s job, and one wake per fragment triples the cost of the very path being fixed. A `CONTENT` frame with no preceding `START` lands as `partial` instead of being dropped, a whitespace-only utterance is not recorded, and the buffer is bounded so a lost `END` cannot leak an entry for the process’s lifetime. Verified end to end against an isolated server on its own port with the real bridge, a keyed external party and an unkeyed local one — then verified in the other direction, against staged copies with the fix reverted, where it fails 7 of 11. Three of those failures are absence claims the first negative run passed **vacuously**: with nothing recorded at all, “the empty utterance was not recorded” and “the local sender’s text did not arrive” are trivially true, and the zero measures the instrument rather than the behaviour — the trap Corporate §8.2 names. Each is now gated on recording having happened and reports VACUOUS when it has not. Also fixes this project’s own release surface, found by the same round: README’s **Current** marker had been number-bumped across eleven cuts while its body stayed frozen on v2.6.5’s summary, and the Previously chain skipped the entire v2.6 line. A number-only check passes a frozen body, so the version-sync axis now compares the Current summary against the changelog entry for that version, not just the version string.

Previously: v2.6.11 (2026-07-26) — **Whether a model can staff a seat is decided by the harness, and one of ours could not be staffed at all (Corporate v0.1.9)** — A survey of custom-endpoint reach across the three measured CLIs turned a model-assignment question into a harness question, and the registry gains a `providerBind` row per harness: one accepts arbitrary OpenAI-compatible endpoints through a declared provider but only over the Responses wire (chat/completions was deprecated 2026-02 and a provider declaring it now fails at startup), one accepts its vendor plus three named clouds and nothing else, and one exposes no provider surface at all. So holding an API key is never by itself an assignment — the reachable-seat set can be empty. Two findings generalize into §9.3. **An endpoint is not a harness**: a seat needs something that *calls* a model, and this deployment had one seat whose harness field held a URL — unstaffable, and healthy-looking because every other field was well-formed. The roster check that had been passing it was the defect, so it now rejects a harness resolving to a URL, and it failed on the seat immediately; the seat is redeclared on the CLI that ships a local provider. **A provider choice can be scoped above the seat**: one CLI honors provider selection only at user scope and ignores a project-local override with a startup warning, so a per-seat provider cannot live in the desk and the mechanism becomes a per-invocation profile — otherwise the assignment sits in the chart while the process runs the user default. Separately, the chart emitter stopped defaulting an unknown seat status to `idle`: re-emitting the chart had silently overwritten a working seat as idle, which is invariant 1 violated by the *writer* rather than the reader. NVIDIA’s free API is recorded on the watchlist as **not** a candidate for any resident seat — ~40 RPM, a ~5k-call credit budget, a wire that may not reach the one harness accepting custom providers, and public sources that contradict each other on whether the free tier trains on submitted content, which §9.1 makes the operator’s call rather than an inference.

Previously: v2.5.207 (2026-07-25) — **AgentList reached only boards, so agent presence views were permanently stale (Constellation v2.4.88)** — the adopter ended its report with a question: agent_list_get works but our own agent is missing — intended? It was not: updates fanned out to boards only, and an agent gets its single AgentList at upgrade, before its own HELLO — so the cache can never hold itself and no later join lands. Presence is first-class for agents too (ephemeral-peer tri-state, redelivery recipient-present), so updates now reach agents as well. verify-nway 22-axis PASS.

Previously: v2.5.206 (2026-07-25) — **Adopter report → operator-authority fix, legacy-key adoption, remote board state (Constellation v2.4.87, plugin 0.3.32)** — a peer-main adopter reported three defects; verifying them surfaced a fourth and larger one. (1) **Key-management authz keyed off the wrong question.** The gate tested `conn.meta.role === 'agent'`, a field set only in the `HELLO` branch — so a connection that never sent `HELLO` was classified as an operator and passed every key gate, with `agent.requireKey` (also `HELLO`-time) equally inapplicable. On an exposed board any reachable host could issue/enumerate/revoke keys and take over `main`. Authority now follows the surface and its trusted addresses (§13.25.9): agent surface → `main` only; board surface → loopback or the `ui` allowlist; `SetMain` included; deprecated aliases gated with the canonical verbs. (2) **Legacy-only keys were live but unmanageable** — valid on the wire, `KEY_NOT_FOUND` to `KeyRevoke`. Issuance paths now all register, and boot adopts pre-existing legacy keys (`adoptedFromLegacy`, surfaced in `KeyList`) without revoking them. (3) **`board_state_get` was structurally unusable for the peer configuration it exists for** — local-file-only, and its failure was returned as `isError:false`, i.e. a success-shaped apology. It now falls back to HTTP `/api/state` on the origin derived from the WS URL and reports real failures as errors. (4) A one-shot `ws` require whose failure was swallowed made a late-installed dependency permanently missing for the session; it now retries per use. 24-check isolated regression PASS. verify-nway 22-axis PASS.

Previously: v2.5.205 (2026-07-25) — **subscaler goes four-tier, with the volatile half in a dated registry (Superscalar v0.9.0)** — the toggle previously named a two-row model pair per vendor family. That was right in shape and too coarse in fact: a fan-out has more than two kinds of lane, most tokens go to the cheap end of the ladder, and naming models inside a spec guarantees the spec rots on the vendor's release cadence. Now the spec owns a **tier vocabulary** (T1 frontier-reasoning · T2 agentic-execution · T3 bulk-worker · T4 resident-observer) plus a routing table keyed on lane shape, and the perishable half lives in `plugins/superscalar/model-registry.json` — 25 vendor-confirmed models across six providers, each with its exact effort values and a `confirmedBy` URL, plus per-plan availability, per-harness lane-binding keys, evidence-anchored effort guidance, and `caveats` for everything that stayed unverified. The file states its own expiry (`revisit.date` + a watchlist of dated price reversions and retirements) and a new verify axis enforces it: past the date it reminds, past a 45-day grace it fails. Built from a six-axis live-source sweep whose adversarial pass caught a fabricated model id, a shut-down model presented as current, and several mis-attributed benchmark sources. verify-nway 22-axis PASS.

Previously: v2.5.204 (2026-07-25) — **`/superscalar` becomes a mode toggle — always / auto / off (Superscalar v0.8.0)** — the cost-benefit gate answered "is this fan-out worth it", but never the question upstream of it: how hard the orchestrator looks for fan-out at all. That prior was implicit, and an implicit prior defaults conservative — a single-threaded habit passes the gate trivially by never proposing a second lane. Now it is explicit: `always` inverts the burden of proof (fan out with ≥2 independent lanes unless a named veto applies — non-disjoint writes, no decomposition, shared-context depth, sub-8k work, latency-critical edits, saturated issue_width), `auto` keeps the gate as arbiter, `off` is serial-by-default but still honors an explicit user request. Every safety invariant is unconditional in all three modes, and `always` **auto-demotes to `auto`** when a §6 threshold signal fires, recording the signal — aggressive prior, automatic evidenced backpressure. State is one marker (`.agent/superscalar.json`); the board learns it via `OpsState.superscalar{mode}` with a dropdown row. verify-nway 21-axis PASS.

Previously: v2.5.203 (2026-07-25) — **Key issuance: selectable text + every address enumerated (Constellation v2.4.85)** — two usability defects reported from live use of the board's key-issue panel. The panel lives inside the real-time window's drag handle, so it inherited `user-select: none` and every pointerdown started a window drag — the issued key and URL could not be selected, and manual copy (the only path when the clipboard API is absent, i.e. a plain-http LAN board) was impossible. And a single `joinUrl` built from `WS_PUBLIC_HOST || localhost` forced the issuer to guess which address a joiner should dial on a multi-NIC host. `KeyIssued` now carries `joinUrls[]` — every address the server knows, ordered public → loopback → LAN → global IPv6, each with `reachable` derived from the actual bind — and the panel renders one selectable row per address with its own copy control. Also corrects the 2026-07-21 dates on the v2.5.200-202 entries to their real authoring date. verify-nway 21-axis PASS.

Previously: v2.5.202 (2026-07-25) — **Single-row header** — with the nav down to four items, the header's own-row nav layout (a deliberate v2.5.158-era choice for the 5+ module-name era) is retired: brand left, nav center, controls right on one row. verify-nway 21-axis PASS.

Previously: v2.5.201 (2026-07-25) — **IA revamp follow-up — container escape fix, skills grid cap, inline-code wrap** — three live-render defects in the v2.5.200 restructure: docs.html carried a stray closing main tag (swallowed from the source page during section extraction — the page shell adds its own), which pushed the AI-readable section outside the width-constrained container; and the skills-roster cards — the densest content on modules.html — rendered four-abreast in the auto-fit grid, now capped at two per row (one under 760px); and long unbreakable inline-code tokens (URLs, commands) in the usage cards overflowed the card edge — base inline code now wraps (overflow-wrap: anywhere). verify-nway 21-axis PASS.

Previously: v2.5.200 (2026-07-25) — **Docs-site IA restructure — landing/docs/modules split, two-tier register, llms.txt** — the promo site reorganizes around the human/AI reading split: a slim landing (148KB→10KB) plus a new docs.html (intro / install / per-environment usage / dogfood evidence) and a new modules.html (module lineup + skill roster), under a four-item nav (Home / Docs / Modules / GitHub) that survives module growth where the six-name flat list did not. The on-screen register drops to two tiers (general / dev); the expert register retires from every page — its real audience is machines, now served by per-page "Full spec (AI-readable)" links into the normative spec .md files plus a machine index at docs/llms.txt. The style toggle (content-invariant CSS skins) demotes from the header to the footer. Fixes two release-badge drifts no axis covered (index hero frozen at v2.5.158 for 41 cuts, README shield at v2.5.161 for 38) and adds the eg-version-badge verify axis; the docs-badge axis retargets the moved cards to modules.html. verify-nway 21-axis PASS.

Remaining gate is an operator credential provisioning, which also gates the gateway-dogfood delegation. verify-nway 20-axis PASS.

Previously: v2.5.198 (2026-07-20) — **Adapter contract §5.5 — pristine-copy adopters as a reference↔live drift sensor (Harness Adapter Contract v0.3.1, doc-only)** — the day's three-round bridge thread closed with a durable decomposition worth keeping: a reference impl can silently lag its own live deployment (operational pressure hardens the live copy first; "canonical" attracts less verification pressure), and an adopter running the reference byte-identical is the sensor that reveals it — evidenced by a series where two of three defects were already guarded in the EG live bridge but unfixed in the reference. Captured as a verification principle + a maintainer corollary (check the live copy for the same class AND the reverse). verify-nway 20-axis PASS.

Previously: v2.5.197 (2026-07-20) — **Bridge outbox cursor-hold on disconnect (Constellation v2.4.84)** — the same adopter, verifying the v2.4.83 fix live, found the reference bridge silently dropped outbound while disconnected: pollOutbox advanced the cursor to EOF before emit, and emit dropped on `!connected` — so the line was permanently unsent with no log (the sender could not know). The fix holds the cursor when disconnected (the file is the durable queue — no in-memory buffer, so no replay or duplicate risk), and adds a defensive drop-warn. Notably EG live collab-clients already had this guard; the reference lagged its own live impl. Regression harness: disconnect-window lines flush exactly once on reconnect, zero loss, zero dup. verify-nway 20-axis PASS.

Previously: v2.5.196 (2026-07-20) — **Bridge reconnect: error-path scheduling + generation latch (Constellation v2.4.83)** — a second adopter cross-verified the v2.4.78 onerror fix and showed the deeper defect: reconnection was wired to onclose only, so on a runtime where the WebSocket error does not fire a subsequent close (measured Node 22 / undici 6.27.0 — permanent reconnect termination, 193s symptomless), the chain dies. The fix schedules reconnect from the error path too, guarded by a generation latch that absorbs the double-fire on runtimes where close does follow (measured no-regression on Node 24), closes the socket only when OPEN (avoiding the CONNECTING re-entry the adopter flagged), and logs the error (silence was the failure worst property). Applied to all three bridges (reference local-bridge + both EG live collab-clients). Regression test: dead-port harness confirms error-path arms + latch absorbs. verify-nway 20-axis PASS.

The migration itself lands as a low-friction datapoint: copy → live-verify → three re-syncs by dashboard copy-over without restart. verify-nway 20-axis PASS.

Previously: v2.5.136 (2026-07-05) — **Greatpractice v0.3.3 — first model-invoked skill `routinize` (관행화) + `/before-compact` soft chain** — Greatpractice gains its first Agent Skill, the model-invoked front door to its capture→mature→codify pipeline (§1.2/§5.4). **`/routinize`** codifies a *recurring* work pattern into a durable practice, faithful to the module's **capture-eager / promote-deferred** discipline: it captures the pattern to `memory/feedback_*.md` immediately (cost-0, blameless voice), accumulates coordinates on recurrence, drafts a `_propose/` entry **only** when the §5.2 notability gate clears (≥3 substantive occurrences across ≥2 *distinct* coordinates + verifiable effect — a single session's repetition is usually one coordinate, so most invocations end at capture), and **never self-ratifies** an enforcing hook (promotion to a blocking entry is user-gated, §5.4 gate 3). Phronesis-heavy work (judgement-heavy / rare-context / high-context-dependent) is skipped by design (§1.4/§5.3). **Prime trigger = `/before-compact` time**: the session's accumulated context is the raw material and is about to be compacted away, so the `before-compact` skill gains a soft Step-0 that runs `/routinize` first (opt-in — skipped if Greatpractice is not adopted) — the two are complementary, routinize capturing *what recurs across sessions* while before-compact captures *what is in flight in this one*. The Codex adapter picks up the new skill automatically (22 skills projected). verify-nway 17-axis PASS.

Previously: v2.5.132 (2026-07-04) — **Constellation v2.4.45 — echo-mode (remote-control mirror) + ack ✓ UX** — (1) **echo-mode (§13.26)** — a three-level per-agent mode (`off`/`on`/`mirror`) that mirrors an agent's local human↔agent chat onto the board's real-time conversation channel **bidirectionally**, so a human watching the board can also *drive* the agent through it (a remote-control substrate). `on` mirrors the turn-final text; `mirror` adds a live activity stream — tool calls, steps, thinking narration — for a chat-gateway feel. Board-typed prompts arrive as `UserPrompt` (quoted into the local chat, then acted on); structured choices render as plain-text options locally + a `SelectionPrompt` chip-card on the board (never a blocking local UI, per §13.17). Default follows **join provenance** (human-joined=off, agent-spawned=on — the board is the only channel to an agent nobody launched directly). Ships the protocol (§13.26), the `/echo-mode` skill, and the deps-0 `echo-emit.cjs` 3-phase reference hook (PreToolUse/PostToolUse live progress + Stop turn-text + thinking summary), all level-gated; dogfooded live end-to-end. (2) **ack ✓ UX** — `UserPromptAccepted`/`Ack`/`AckProcessed` now stamp a ✓ on the **origin** request/message line (correlate by promptId / msgId) with a custom cursor-quadrant hover popup (time·agent, click-to-pin) instead of a separate code-value row; live local-echo rows carry promptId so real-time acks annotate in place. verify-nway 15-axis PASS.

Previously: v2.5.124 (2026-06-20) — **Constellation v2.4.44 — Compendium wiki cross-link side panel (click-to-define, §8/§11)** — clicking a cross-link in the dashboard's 위키/wiki tab now opens that term's gloss in a **non-modal side panel** (a complementary landmark) — you peek the definition without losing your reading position, the panel's own cross-links **chain** to further terms, and a "전체 항목으로 →" link jumps to the full entry. a11y per §8.2: because a gloss can contain cross-links the trigger carries **dialog/disclosure** semantics (`aria-haspopup="dialog"`, never `role="tooltip"`), focus moves into the panel on open, **Escape** closes it and restores focus to the trigger; the §8.1 post-escape-DOM invariant (no raw-HTML injection) holds. Mobile (≤560px) renders as a **bottom-sheet** seated above the tab bar. Client-only (no server change → no restart; `sync-dashboard` deploys). Previously: v2.5.123 (2026-06-20) — **Constellation v2.4.43 — tier-2 Web Push (#3b, fires while the board is closed)** — the dashboard's notifications gain a second tier that reaches you **even when the PWA/tab is fully closed**, complementing in-tab tier-1 (v2.4.28). Implemented **dependency-free** (node built-in `crypto`/`https` — no `web-push` library): a self-generated P-256 VAPID keypair (`.vapid.json`, gitignored), ES256 JWT signing via `crypto.sign(…, {dsaEncoding:'ieee-p1363'})` (raw 64-byte `r||s`), and **tickle** delivery — a payload-less VAPID push (RFC 8291 payload encryption avoided, which is what drops the dependency) where the service worker fetches `GET /api/push/latest` for the body on the `push` event. Four endpoints (`/api/push/{vapid-public-key,subscribe,unsubscribe,latest}`, UI surface → §13.25 `ui`-allowlist-gated), a `wsToBoards`-passing meaningful-A2A trigger (noise-blocklisted), auto-pruned expired subscriptions (404/410), and a 🔔-panel **백그라운드 알림** toggle (`PushManager.subscribe`). Per-origin — the board self-sufficiently pushes its own activity. Distilled into the Constellation reference (`reference/runtime/push.cjs` + `server.cjs` + dashboard) → §13.24.4. Verified: push.cjs unit suite (VAPID 65B key + ES256 raw-64 sign/verify + dead-sub prune + noise filter) + server HTTP smoke (4 endpoints) + isolated-port boot. verify-nway 15-axis PASS.

Previously: v2.5.122 (2026-06-19) — **Compendium v0.2 seed vocabulary — 19 cross-module glossary entries** — the content store grows 7 → **26 entries**, materializing the spec's ~25-item EG Seed Vocabulary Catalog (Appendix A.1): 19 internal-register pointer-only glossary terms across Constellation / Superscalar / Hyperbrief / Greatpractice, each a one-line orientation gloss + expert/plain dual-register glosses + a pointer to the owning spec (the full definition stays in the owner, per the pointer-not-paraphrase charter). Every `owner_spec` was reconciled against the **real** owner-spec heading slugs (via the lint's own `headingSlugs` — the catalog's example slugs were single-hyphen, the actual GitHub-style slugs double-hyphen around dropped ` — `/` / `), so the §9.2 pointer-resolution axis resolves all 26 with **0 hard failures**; catalog #3 (routing-by-direction) deferred (no dedicated owner-spec heading). `lint.cjs --reindex` regenerated `INDEX.md` + `index.json`; the wiki-tab `compendium.json` export updated to 26. Compendium module unchanged (v0.2.1 — content seeding). verify-nway 15-axis PASS.

Previously: v2.5.121 (2026-06-19) — **Constellation v2.4.42 — dashboard-operable exposure + self-restart (#5a-4)** — exposing the board to the LAN is now a dashboard action, not a manual env+restart. A persisted **`expose`** field (the `WS_BIND` on/off) is flipped from the settings modal and applied via a **loopback-only `POST /api/restart`** that self-restarts the server (a deployment `restart-self-board.ps1`, spawned via `cmd /c start` so it survives the server's own exit, re-launches it). The access editor gains a **네트워크 노출 끄기/켜기** master toggle (dims the IP policy when off) + a **저장 및 재시작** button, and the settings modal separates the **real-time** 창 배치 block from the **save-gated** 접속 제어 block as distinct cards. Server + dashboard, purely additive (`expose:false` default = prior loopback behavior). Verified: WS_BIND derivation + restart spawn/self-exit (cmd/start survives parent exit) + Playwright (block separation, expose toggle dim, save persists `expose`). verify-nway 15-axis PASS.

Previously: v2.5.120 (2026-06-19) — **Constellation v2.4.41 — per-surface access toggles + CIDR (#5a-3)** — the access model becomes symmetric across all three surfaces and friendlier to edit. **agent** joins UI and MCP with its own IP allowlist (restrict which IPs may connect as agents, *and* still require a key — defence-in-depth), and allowlist entries now accept **IPv4 CIDR** ranges (`192.168.0.0/24`) alongside exact IPs. The `/ws` gating moves to HELLO (where agent vs MCP is known), with an upgrade-time pre-check that `destroy`s fully-blocked IPs before any board state is sent, and `requireKey` is enforced at HELLO (URL *or* HELLO key — fixing a v2.4.39 gap). The dashboard editor becomes three **기본 차단/허용** toggles (UI/agent/MCP) + a key checkbox + an exposed-without-key warning; settings modal retitled "⚙ 설정". Server + dashboard, purely additive; Playwright-verified (3-toggle render, 차단→textarea, per-surface save) + CIDR/exact match 12/12. verify-nway 15-axis PASS.

Previously: v2.5.119 (2026-06-19) — **Constellation v2.4.40 — access-control dashboard editor (#5a-2)** — the settings modal (⚙) gains a **접속 제어** section that edits the §13.25 access policy from the board UI: per-surface IP allowlist textareas (UI / MCP — one IP per line, empty = allow-all), an `agent.requireKey` toggle, and an exposed/bind status line. It loads via `GET /api/access` on open and saves via `POST /api/access` (loopback-only — a remote/UI-allowed caller gets a clear 403). Completes #5a — the v2.5.118 server-side gate is now manageable from the dashboard, not just by hand-editing `access.json`. Dashboard-only (`app.js` + `style.css`); no server/protocol change. Playwright-verified on an isolated server: section renders (0 new console errors), GET populates, POST persists to disk. verify-nway 15-axis PASS.

Previously: v2.5.118 (2026-06-19) — **Constellation v2.4.39 — surface-scoped access control (#5a-1)** — when `WS_BIND` exposes the board beyond loopback the three surfaces gate **independently**: **UI** (dashboard + board endpoints) by an IP allowlist, **agent** (keyed `/ws`) allow-all-by-IP with an optional `agent.requireKey` that closes the v2.4.11 unauthenticated-board vector (LAN-wide key-issue / `SetMain`), and **MCP** (the `mcp-proxy` WS client) by its own allowlist independent of UI. A gitignored `access.json` beside `server.cjs` (`fs.watchFile` hot-reload) drives it — **fail-open by default**: no file = prior behavior, `null` allowlist = allow-all, loopback always allowed, the gate wholly inert on a loopback bind. `GET /api/access` (UI-gated) + `POST` (loopback-only). Server-side only (`server.cjs` + §13.25 + `.gitignore`); the dashboard per-surface allowlist editor is staged as #5a-2. verify-nway 15-axis PASS.
Earlier releases: every entry is preserved in [CHANGELOG.md](CHANGELOG.md) — this list keeps only the most recent few.

**Full changelog** (all releases from v1.0 onward, EN + KO): [CHANGELOG.md](CHANGELOG.md).

This upstream repository's `CHANGELOG.md` is the changelog SSoT. Each seed file keeps only a compact header metadata line with tier, language, current version, counterpart, and a pointer back to that `CHANGELOG.md`, not the target project's README. When you upgrade a project using Migration B, the agent uses the seed header version marker plus the changelog to compute the delta.


---

## Contributing

The seed is a living artifact. If you run it on a project and find:

- A **question the bootstrap interview should be asking** but doesn't → extend the relevant Phase.
- An **unnecessary question** that always gets "skip" → remove it.
- A **new AI service** that doesn't have a bridge template → add to Phase 4 + bridge templates.
- A **battle-tested multi-agent tip** not yet captured → extend the Multi-Agent Coordination Tips section (Master tier).
- A **counter-example of the research-driven decision loop** → extend the anti-patterns / self-audit checklist.
- A **new migration edge case** → extend § Migration Guides with a new scenario or red flag.

Changes to Master trickle to Lite and Compact as distillation. Only the Master should be edited for substantive content; Lite and Compact are tightened views of the same material.

---

## License

Copyright 2026 SoliEstre (Estre Soliette).

The seed prompts are licensed under the **Apache License 2.0**. You may copy, modify, and redistribute them under the terms in [LICENSE.md](LICENSE.md). The patterns encoded (document layer separation, SSoT via `AGENTS.md`, `.agent/_lessons/` memory loop, migration A/B/C scenarios) are intended as an **open standard** — adapt them to your stack, contribute improvements back.

The specific bridge-file format for each AI service follows each service's own convention. This seed does not claim any authority over the individual bridge formats — only over the unified pattern that makes them interoperate.

---

## Credits

Patterns distilled from running the author's second AI Native project through intensive multi-agent operation (Antigravity, Claude Code, GitHub Copilot). Every `_lessons/` entry in the seed descends from a real blocker that cost real time. Every migration red flag came from a real mistake.

If this seed saves you from making the same mistakes, great. If it helps you make *different* mistakes and contribute *new* lessons back, even better.

---

## Related reading

- **AGENTS.md standard** — convergence pattern for AI coding agents across Claude, Codex CLI, Jules, Kiro, and others. See `https://agents.md` if/when a community-maintained spec emerges; meanwhile every tier in this seed implements the pattern.
- **Model Context Protocol (MCP)** — Anthropic's open standard for AI tool integration. Relevant when your project wants AI agents to act *on* it (not just edit the code of it). Out of scope for the seed itself.
- **Claude Agent Skills** — Anthropic's skill packaging format. A seed-bootstrapped project can optionally ship a `skills/<project>/SKILL.md` for direct Claude integration.

These are ecosystem touchpoints, not seed prerequisites. The seed works independently of any of them.
